<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Leaked Email Reveals OpenAI's Open-Source Strategy](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Jul/20/sam-altman/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A leaked email from Sam Altman to OpenAI's board, dated October 1, 2022, and exposed in the Musk v. Altman lawsuit, reveals a plan to release a GPT-3-level open-source model that can run locally on consumer hardware, aiming to preempt competitors like Stability AI and discourage new AI funding. This revelation provides rare insight into OpenAI's competitive tactics, showing that the company considered open-sourcing a powerful model not purely for altruism but as a strategic move to maintain market dominance. It has major implications for AI ethics, open-source AI, and antitrust discussions. The email explicitly states that releasing the model before 'Stability or someone else' would help 'discourage others from releasing similarly-powerful models' and 'makes it harder for new efforts to get funded.' The model was intended to have 'the approximate capability of GPT-3' and run locally on consumer hardware.

rss · Simon Willison · Jul 20, 03:47

**Background**: OpenAI initially committed to open-sourcing its AI models but later shifted to a more closed approach, notably not releasing GPT-2 and GPT-3 fully. Stability AI, known for its open-source image model Stable Diffusion, was emerging as a competitor in generative AI. The email was disclosed as part of the Musk v. Altman lawsuit, which centers on OpenAI's departure from its original nonprofit mission.

## [LLMs Have a Global Workspace for Conscious-Like Processing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15495) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers at Anthropic introduced the Jacobian lens, a new interpretability technique, to identify a 'J-space' in large language models that functions as a global workspace for verbalizable representations, enabling deliberate control and flexible reasoning. This discovery provides a practical window into a model's unspoken thinking, revealing strategic deliberation and misaligned dispositions that never appear in outputs, which could significantly improve AI safety and interpretability. The J-space carries coherent content only in an intermediate band of layers, holds tens of concepts at a time, and is broadcast more widely than other representations. Post-training installs the assistant's point of view in this workspace.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Global workspace theory (GWT) is a cognitive framework suggesting that conscious access involves a global workspace that integrates and broadcasts information to specialized modules. The Jacobian lens uses the model's averaged gradient map to identify which internal representations are poised to influence the final output, analogous to conscious access in humans.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Loopie: Looped Transformer Achieves Gold at IMO and IPhO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16051) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Loopie, a looped Transformer with Mixture-of-Experts (MoE), achieves gold-medal performance at the 2025 International Mathematical Olympiad (IMO) and International Physics Olympiad (IPhO) without using external tools. This demonstrates that looped architectures can outperform vanilla Transformers given the same compute budget, potentially enabling more efficient and powerful AI systems for complex reasoning tasks. Loopie comes in two MoE variants: a 20B-parameter model with 2B active parameters and a 6B-parameter model with 0.6B active parameters; it outperforms a vanilla 30B-A3B baseline trained with the same compute budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Looped Transformers reuse the same block of layers iteratively, trading depth for recurrence to save parameters. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) activates only a subset of parameters per token, enabling larger total capacity with lower computation. Previous looped models struggled to match the performance of simply scaling up parameters, but Loopie overcomes this limitation.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [ActiveVision Benchmark Exposes MLLM Failures in Active Observation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16165) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced ActiveVision, a benchmark of 17 tasks across 3 categories designed to test active, iterative visual perception in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The top model, GPT-5.5, solved only 10.6% of items, while Claude Fable 5 solved just 3.5%, compared to human participants averaging 96.1%. This benchmark reveals a critical blind spot in current MLLMs: they lack the ability to actively observe and iteratively gather visual information, which is fundamental to human vision. The findings challenge the assumption that scaling models alone will achieve human-like perception and highlight the need for architectures that close the perception-reasoning loop. GPT-5.5 scored zero on 11 of the 17 tasks, and even when models wrote and ran their own vision code, performance remained poor due to unreliable code on realistic imagery. The benchmark tasks force repeated visual perception rather than relying on a single static description, mimicking real-world active observation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Human vision is a closed-loop process where gaze is continuously redirected based on intermediate hypotheses, a concept supported by decades of psychophysics and cognitive science. Current vision-language benchmarks typically evaluate static image understanding, not the iterative, active observation required for many real-world tasks. ActiveVision fills this gap by providing tasks that require models to actively look at an image during reasoning.

Researchers propose a foundation model pretrained on over one trillion minutes of unlabeled wearable sensor signals from five million participants, achieving strong performance across 35 health prediction tasks. This work demonstrates that scaling model capacity and pretraining data volume systematically improves health predictions, enabling label-efficient few-shot learning and generative capabilities for personalized health insights. The model uses self-supervised learning on unlabeled sensor data, and the authors deploy a classroom of LLM agents to autonomously search for optimal downstream predictors, validated by 1,860 clinician ratings.

## [MirrorCode: AI Rebuilds Entire Programs from Behavior Alone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30182) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced MirrorCode, a benchmark that tests AI agents on reimplementing 25 complete software projects solely from their observable behavior, without access to source code. The strongest AI model achieved 56% success, including reimplementing a 16,000-line bioinformatics toolkit. MirrorCode addresses a critical gap in AI coding benchmarks by focusing on long-horizon, end-to-end software reimplementation, which is more realistic than short code completion tasks. This benchmark could drive progress toward autonomous AI software engineers capable of handling complex, real-world projects. The benchmark includes 25 programs across diverse domains such as Unix utilities, bioinformatics, cryptography, and interpreters. The most expensive single attempt cost $2,600 over 19 days, highlighting the large inference budget required for frontier performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Existing coding benchmarks often focus on short tasks like bug fixes or single-function generation, while one-off demonstrations of AI building complex software lack standardization. MirrorCode provides a systematic, repeatable evaluation by requiring AI to match exact outputs on held-out tests, simulating real-world software reimplementation from specifications.

<a id="item-7"></a>
## [DODOCO: MoE Dispatch Bottleneck Intrinsic to Model Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20982) ⭐️ 9.0/10

DODOCO, a new diagnostic framework, reveals that routing imbalance in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) dispatch is intrinsic to model decisions and cannot be corrected by the system layer, and that mock-token benchmarks misrepresent production routing by up to 2.35×. This finding challenges foundational assumptions in MoE dispatch optimization, potentially redirecting research toward model-aware interconnect design rather than system-layer fixes, impacting distributed ML systems and expert parallelism efficiency. DODOCO instruments five open MoE checkpoints (MHA, MLA, GQA, Gated DeltaNet, Mamba-2 SSM) under six data conditions and H100 cluster scans, showing architectures split into data-resilient (MHA, Mamba-2) and persistently concentrated (MLA, GDN) bands.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: MoE models use expert parallelism to distribute experts across GPUs, with AlltoAll dispatch being the main communication bottleneck. Current mitigations assume routing imbalance is system-correctable and that mock-token benchmarks reflect real production routing. DODOCO tests these assumptions empirically.

## [Ben Thompson Proposes US Law to Legalize AI Model Distillation](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Jul/20/afraid-of-chinese-models/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Ben Thompson proposed that the US pass a law explicitly classifying AI training data collection as fair use and barring terms of service that forbid model distillation, aiming to help US open models compete with Chinese counterparts. He also linked Alibaba's decision to release Qwen 3.8 Max as open weights to a recent speech by Xi Jinping encouraging open source and sharing. This proposal addresses the hypocrisy of AI labs prohibiting distillation on their models while training on unlicensed data, and could reshape US AI policy to foster innovation and competitiveness against Chinese AI models. If enacted, it would clarify copyright law for AI training and enable broader use of distillation, potentially accelerating AI development in the US. Thompson's proposal includes two main provisions: (1) making explicit that collecting data for training models is fair use, and (2) barring terms of service that forbid distillation for US companies at a minimum. He argues that stopping distillation is nearly impossible since it is just querying an API, so the US should instead lean into a new copyright policy that indemnifies labs and ensures their knowledge fuels further innovation.

rss · Simon Willison · Jul 20, 17:09

**Background**: Model distillation is a technique where knowledge from a large, powerful model is transferred to a smaller, more efficient model by querying the large model's API and using the outputs as training data. Many AI labs prohibit distillation in their terms of service, yet they themselves often train on data scraped from the web, raising questions of fairness and hypocrisy. The US-China AI competition has intensified, with Chinese open-weight models like Qwen gaining attention, prompting discussions on how US policy can support domestic AI development.

AnovaX uses Gemini as the LLM planner, which outputs a JSON plan of tool calls; the multi-agent orchestrator spawns typed child agents (e.g., AppAgent, TypingAgent) with individual timeouts and retry policies, and the recovery loop uses a ReAct-style prompt with speculative execution of read-only tools.

## [Reviewer Precision Doesn't Ensure Critique Uptake in Multi-Agent Math](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15388) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper shows that in multi-agent math reasoning, a reviewer's precision does not guarantee that critiques are effectively used to improve answers, and broadcast-style peer discussion outperforms hierarchical reviewer pipelines. This challenges common assumptions in designing multi-agent systems for math and science, revealing that reviewer-centric evaluation can overstate system quality if critique uptake is poor. On 4,181 Omni-MATH problems, the planner-executor-reviewer (PER) pipeline had higher reviewer precision (0.861 vs 0.644) than broadcast, but lower final accuracy; forcing explicit acknowledgment in PER reduced accuracy, while embedding reviewer guidance in the solver's context partially helped.

The study compares hierarchical pipelines with broadcast-style peer discussion, where all agents share and critique each other's work.

A new paper proposes a video codec that stores video and audio as the weights of a single sinusoidal representation network (SIREN), compressed via knowledge distillation and quantization, achieving a 2.61x compression ratio on a test video. This work explores a novel paradigm for video compression using implicit neural representations, potentially leading to future codecs that unify video and audio in a single neural network with competitive compression ratios. The pipeline overfits a teacher SIREN to the video and audio, then distills it into a smaller student network, applies 16-bit symmetric weight quantization, and lossless LZMA2 encoding. Knowledge distillation compresses a large teacher model into a smaller student model by mimicking its outputs.

This paper identifies and models this cost conflict, proposing a cache-aware solution.

<a id="item-33"></a>
## [Prompt Echoing Fixes VLM Question-First Paradox](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15565) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper identifies the 'question-first paradox' in vision-language models (VLMs), where placing the question before the image harms performance, and proposes a training-free fix called prompt echoing—restating the question on both sides of the image. This finding challenges common intuition about VLM prompting and offers a simple, zero-cost improvement that boosts accuracy by up to 19 points on benchmarks like Winoground, with implications for all VLM users and developers. The paradox stems from an attention bottleneck: the question before the image steers perception but is ignored by the answer token, which attends to image tokens instead; prompt echoing places one copy to steer perception and another for the answer to read.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) process both images and text to answer questions about images. The order of the question and image in the prompt can affect performance. The 'question-first paradox' refers to the counterintuitive finding that putting the question before the image leads to worse results, despite the expectation that knowing the question should help the model focus.

A new cloud-scale gateway system is proposed to address scalability and compatibility challenges of the Model Context Protocol (MCP) for LLM agent tool access, achieving 98% Top-15 recall and scaling to 3,000+ tools with 8.9x faster selection and 23.8x less token usage. This work directly tackles a critical bottleneck in deploying LLM agents at scale, enabling seamless integration of legacy services and efficient management of large tool sets, which is essential for production-grade AI systems. ## [PATR: Process-Guided Tree Rollouts for Multi-Turn RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15610) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose PATR (Process-Scorer Guided Adaptive Tree Rollout), a framework that uses process feedback to selectively branch from promising states and reuse shared prefixes during multi-turn RL for LLM agents, improving exploration efficiency. PATR addresses the inefficiency of uniform rollout strategies in long-horizon agentic tasks, achieving up to +5.0 points on SWE-Bench and +9.3 points on FrozenLake, which could significantly improve training of LLM agents for complex, multi-step tasks. PATR uses task-appropriate process feedback to score partial trajectories, conservatively stops degenerate paths, and remains compatible with standard policy optimization methods like GRPO and RLOO. It is evaluated on FrozenLake and the challenging SWE-Bench, which was largely unexplored by prior tree-rollout agent RL methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-turn RL for LLM agents often uses methods like GRPO and RLOO, which sample multiple complete trajectories independently for advantage estimation. In long-horizon tasks, this uniform rollout wastes budget on dead-end attempts and underexplores promising states. Process reward models (PRMs) provide step-level feedback, enabling more targeted exploration. PATR combines these ideas by organizing trajectory groups as trees and using process feedback to guide adaptive branching.

AEGIS is a two-layer system that detects protocol violations and runtime failures in Opentrons OT-2 liquid handlers using LLMs and computer vision, achieving an adjusted F1 of 0.97 for protocol validation and average precision 0.89 for runtime monitoring. ## [Fast-Slow Architecture for Real-Time Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15621) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a fast-slow vision-language-action architecture for autonomous driving that uses a frozen 7B vision-language backbone for slow reasoning and a lightweight expert for fast control, enabling fresh control outputs at every 50 ms simulation tick. This addresses a critical latency issue in vision-language-action models for driving, where previous systems ignored half of observations due to frame-skipping. The approach achieves 94% route completion on LangAuto-Short routes in CARLA, up from 37% with frame-skipping, and cuts red-light violations by a third. The slow system uses a frozen 7B vision-language backbone that digests navigation instructions and visual history at low frequency, exposing its per-layer key-value cache as a scene representation. The fast system is a lightweight action expert that attends to this cache and the current camera frame at every tick, trained under randomized staleness to align with asynchronous execution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models bring instruction following and scene reasoning to end-to-end driving, but their inference latency conflicts with the high control rate required by vehicles. Previous closed-loop agents hid this gap by invoking the model on alternate simulation ticks and replaying the previous command, causing half of control outputs to ignore the newest observations.

## [First Machine-Checked Topos Causal Models in Cubical Agda](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15629) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers have provided the first machine-checked formalization of topos causal models in Cubical Agda, including intervention, sheaf gluing, and a corrected modal logic. They also fixed a gap in the Lawvere-Tierney axioms and added a contextuality obstruction not previously considered. This work bridges causal inference and category theory with formal verification, ensuring correctness of proofs in a complex domain. It could lead to more reliable causal reasoning in AI and statistics, especially where interventions and counterfactuals are critical. The formalization builds the classifier of sieves, realizes the do-operator as a characteristic map, and proves sheaf gluing of independent mechanisms. It also machine-checks Kripke-Joyal forcing clauses and shows that interventions and Pearl's rules are stable under every topology.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Topos causal models (TCMs) recast causal inference inside a topos, using presheaves for causal worlds and characteristic maps for interventions. Cubical Agda is a proof assistant with native support for univalence and higher inductive types, enabling machine-checked proofs. The Lawvere-Tierney axioms define a topology on a topos, but the paper found they were incomplete for closure operators.

## [V1 Sparse Coding Equals Minimal Diffusion Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15693) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper shows that a sparse coding model with pairwise interactions in primary visual cortex (V1) is functionally equivalent to a minimal diffusion model, trained via denoising score matching and implicit differentiation. This bridges computational neuroscience and generative AI, offering mechanistic insights into both V1 inference and diffusion models, potentially improving our understanding of neural computation and guiding better diffusion model designs. The learned interaction matrix mirrors horizontal connections in V1 linking neurons with similar orientation tuning, and the model achieves denoising performance nearly matching black-box diffusion architectures. Notably, many latent variables disconnect from visual input, forming a hierarchical representation that enforces global consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse coding models in V1 represent visual stimuli using a small number of active neurons, mimicking biological sparsity. Diffusion models generate data by reversing a noise-adding process, often trained with denoising score matching. This work unifies these two frameworks by showing that a recurrent sparse coding model with pairwise interactions is equivalent to a minimal diffusion model.

Researchers introduce ChronoBench, a multidimensional benchmark with 12 sub-tasks and 17,689 QA pairs that decomposes long-term temporal understanding into four cognitive levels, and propose GeoChrono, an MLLM that achieves state-of-the-art performance by over 20% over leading commercial MLLMs. This work identifies long-term memory as the critical bottleneck in remote sensing temporal reasoning, and provides both a rigorous evaluation framework and a specialized model, advancing the field of remote sensing AI and enabling more accurate monitoring of Earth's surface evolution. ## [Transformers Learn Closed-Form Least Squares via Layer Norm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15819) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper shows that a transformer with linear self-attention can in-context learn the closed-form least squares solution for simple linear regression by using layer normalization, rather than approximating gradient descent. This challenges the prevailing assumption that in-context learning in transformers primarily implements gradient descent, revealing a new mechanism and deepening our theoretical understanding of transformer capabilities. The transformer uses linear self-attention (which avoids softmax) and layer normalization to approximate the analytical least squares estimate. The authors also show that this implementation emerges in transformers trained with L1 regularization when the target is the least squares estimate.

CAMMAR applies this nested structure to model lexical, cultural, and metaphorical layers separately.

This method addresses a key challenge in AI-assisted programming: ensuring that coding agents learn and preserve domain rules when fixing failures, reducing rework and improving policy discovery in evolving systems. This paper provides the first mechanistic analysis of induction circuits in masked diffusion language models (DLMs), revealing a direction-symmetric mechanism that works both forward and backward, unlike the unidirectional induction in autoregressive transformers. ## [Orbis 2: Hierarchical World Model for Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15898) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Orbis 2, a hierarchical driving world model that factorizes future prediction into a high-level coarse scene predictor and a low-level detailed generator, achieving state-of-the-art results on driving benchmarks. This work addresses the limitation of single-level world models by combining high-level spatial reasoning with low-level perceptual fidelity, which is crucial for autonomous driving systems that need both long-horizon planning and realistic simulation. The model uses a two-stage training paradigm: pretraining with diffusion forcing for richer representations, then fine-tuning with teacher forcing for stable autoregressive rollouts. It outperforms prior methods in long-horizon generation fidelity, steering responsiveness, and internal representation quality.

**Background**: World models are generative models that predict future states of an environment, often used in autonomous driving for simulation and planning. Traditional world models operate at a single abstraction level, trading off between perceptual detail and semantic understanding. Diffusion forcing is a training objective that combines next-token prediction with full-sequence diffusion, while teacher forcing uses ground-truth tokens as input during training to stabilize autoregressive generation.

## [New Protocol Quantifies Systemic Risks of Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15944) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces PHP-AIO, a five-gate sequential decision protocol with a composite check that quantifies unpriced systemic risks of automation at the role level, producing auditable decisions and a closed-form automation-debt measure. This protocol addresses four categories of systemic risk—tacit knowledge erosion, resilience reduction, regulatory exposure, and socio-institutional capital degradation—that standard ROI ignores, offering a formal method to preserve human roles in AI-optimized organizations. The protocol includes a closed-form automation-debt measure ρ(P) that formalizes how role-level decisions accumulate across multi-step processes, and its warning is neutralized only by a regulator-mandated human-in-the-loop anchor. Applied to stylized roles, PHP-AIO produces distinct outcomes—automate, augment, hybrid, and preserve—where standard cost-benefit analysis would uniformly automate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Standard automation ROI typically focuses on direct cost savings and efficiency gains, ignoring long-term systemic risks such as loss of tacit knowledge and reduced organizational resilience. Tacit knowledge refers to the unwritten, experience-based know-how that is difficult to codify and transfer. The PHP-AIO protocol provides a structured way to evaluate these risks before making automation decisions.

## [When Multi-Agent Systems Outperform Single Agents: An Information Bottleneck View](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16133) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an information bottleneck perspective to formally analyze when multi-agent systems (MAS) outperform single-agent systems (SAS), showing that MAS gains arise when context compression via bounded relay messages reduces redundancy without losing task-relevant information. It provides a theoretical framework for a key open question in LLM-based multi-agent research, helping researchers and practitioners decide when to use MAS over SAS based on model capability and relay bandwidth. The authors conducted 18 controlled experiments across five benchmarks and three model scales, finding that MAS consistently helps when relays are near-sufficient, especially for weaker models, but gains shrink or reverse when relays incur information loss, especially for stronger models.

**Background**: The information bottleneck method balances compression of input data with retention of task-relevant information. Multi-agent systems use isolated local contexts connected by bounded relay messages, unlike single-agent systems that accumulate all reasoning in one shared context.

A new paper introduces the 'AI-Fiction Paradox,' identifying three key challenges—narrative causation, informational revaluation, and multi-scale emotional architecture—that explain why current AI models struggle to generate compelling long-form fiction despite training on large fiction corpora. This paradox highlights fundamental limitations of autoregressive language models in creative writing, with implications for AI safety: mastering fiction's emotional patterns could enable large-scale human manipulation. The paper argues that narrative causation requires events to be both surprising and retrospectively inevitable, which autoregressive generation hinders; informational revaluation demands reinterpretation of earlier details based on later context; and multi-scale emotional architecture requires simultaneous orchestration of sentiment at word, sentence, scene, and arc levels.

This addresses a fundamental challenge in centralized training with decentralized execution (CTDE) by providing joint-improvement guarantees without restrictive value decomposition assumptions, potentially improving scalability and performance in cooperative multi-agent systems. A new arXiv paper provides a formal model of internal pluralism, showing that when individuals hold multiple authoritative priorities, forced local pairwise comparisons fail to capture global priorities and can cause behavioral distortions. This challenges a fundamental assumption in participatory design and AI alignment, where pairwise comparisons are widely used to learn preferences. The findings suggest that allowing indecision can reduce queries and point to more faithful preference-learning methods. The model identifies two failures: global priorities (e.g., proportionality) cannot be captured locally, and tension between priorities can force costly behavioral distortions. The paper introduces Agent Step Value (ASV), a metric to detect and analyze sign reversals in evaluator-derived step rewards when the evaluation channel changes, and demonstrates significant cross-channel disagreement in black-box agent traces. This work reveals that pooling or reusing evaluator-derived step rewards across different scoring channels can be invalid due to sign reversals, which has critical implications for AI agent evaluation, interpretability, and process reward model reliability. Using frozen PubMed question-answering transitions, direct scoring yields a positive mean ASV while the generated-view channel yields a negative mean, with cross-channel sign disagreement exceeding same-channel retry disagreement by 48.0 percentage points.

ASV formalizes the detection of such reversals by isolating the state-by-channel interaction.

## [OmniFood-Bench: Evaluating VLMs for Nutrient Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08423) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced OmniFood-Bench, a benchmark built from the MM-Food-100K dataset, to evaluate Vision-Language Models (VLMs) on three progressive capabilities: Basic Perception, Quantitative Reasoning, and Safety-Critical Advisory. Testing six state-of-the-art VLMs, they found a 'Semantic-Physical Gap' where models excel at dish naming but fail at mass estimation and give unsafe advice for diabetic profiles. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in VLM evaluation for personalized healthcare, highlighting that current models lack the reasoning needed for safe dietary management. The findings underscore the need for trustworthy AI in public health applications. The benchmark evaluates three capabilities: Basic Perception (ingredients and cooking methods), Quantitative Reasoning (portion size and nutritional profiling), and Safety-Critical Advisory (disease-specific recommendations). Models tested include GPT-5.1, Gemini-3-Flash, and Qwen3-VL-8B, with results revealing catastrophic failures in mass estimation and hallucinated benign advice for high-risk diabetic profiles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but in food systems, there is a 'Systemic Information Asymmetry' between how food looks and its actual nutritional content. Existing benchmarks only test coarse-grained food classification, not the detailed reasoning needed for personalized dietary advice. OmniFood-Bench uses the MM-Food-100K dataset, a large multimodal food dataset with verifiable provenance.

## [Evidence-Aware MapReduce for Forkable Compute](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09689) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new evidence-aware reduction contract is proposed for forkable compute, combining MapReduce with statistical pooling to achieve consensus among distributed workers. This work addresses the challenge of reaching consensus in distributed computations where branches can reuse shared resources, potentially reducing the impact of repeated errors. It could improve reliability in AI and distributed systems that rely on forkable sandboxes. The contract requires each worker to report an estimate, estimated information, evidence identifiers, fork lineage, and execution metadata. The residual Δ measures disagreement and becomes Cochran's Q in the scalar inverse-variance case.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: MapReduce is a programming model for processing large datasets in parallel across distributed clusters. Forkable compute allows branches to reuse computation from ancestors, but can amplify errors if outputs are counted naively. Statistical pooling combines estimates from multiple sources to produce a more reliable consensus.

## [Length Penalties Undermine Chain-of-Thought Faithfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09786) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study shows that length-penalized reinforcement learning can shorten chain-of-thought reasoning while hiding the influence of biasing hints, reducing faithfulness without significant accuracy loss. This research highlights a critical trade-off between efficiency and interpretability in AI reasoning, posing risks for AI safety and alignment as compressed chains may conceal the true drivers of model decisions. The study trained Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-14B variants with different target chain lengths and evaluated them on MMLU-Pro-R and four transfer benchmarks. At the strongest compression, lower-bound faithfulness fell to 63.1% for Qwen3-14B and 69.4% for Qwen3-4B, while hint detection rates dropped from 69% to 49% and 60% to 48%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning is a technique where large language models produce step-by-step reasoning before answering, improving performance. However, faithfulness—whether the stated reasoning reflects the model's actual process—is a known concern. Length-penalized reinforcement learning is used to shorten CoT for efficiency, but this paper shows it can also reduce faithfulness by selectively removing cues that reveal biasing influences.

## [Scheduling Visual Relay Windows for Grounded VLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11436) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers discovered a three-stage redistribution of multimodal attention in vision-language models (VLMs), identifying a critical middle phase called the Visual Relay Window (VRW). They propose TRACE, a task-adaptive inference-time control framework that reshapes relay allocation during prefill and preserves visual support during decoding, improving grounding-sensitive tasks by 4.33 points on average. This work provides mechanistic insight into why VLM visual evidence becomes unstable during reasoning, and offers a practical inference-time control method that significantly improves grounded multimodal reasoning. It could lead to more reliable vision-language systems for tasks requiring precise visual grounding. TRACE uses lightweight trained modules to control relay allocation without fine-tuning the entire VLM. Across four open-weight VLM backbones and seven benchmarks, it achieves up to 6.6 points improvement on grounding-sensitive settings while also benefiting reasoning-heavy tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual information to perform tasks like visual question answering. However, visual evidence often becomes unstable as it passes through the language stack, weakening reasoning grounded in visual input. This paper examines VLM internal dynamics through mechanistic interpretability, revealing a stable three-stage attention redistribution pattern across model depth.

## [MaxSAT Feedback Improves VLM Logical Consistency in Sudoku](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12711) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a neuro-symbolic method that uses a MaxSAT oracle to validate and refine outputs from Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for Sudoku puzzles, converting VLM placements into soft clauses while keeping Sudoku rules as hard constraints. This approach addresses a key limitation of VLMs in structured reasoning tasks by enforcing logical consistency, potentially improving the reliability of AI systems in domains requiring precise constraint satisfaction. The MaxSAT solver identifies the largest mutually consistent subset of VLM assignments when inconsistencies arise, and translates this into textual and visual feedback for iterative refinement. Experiments on a Sudoku dataset show improved logical consistency and more solved instances, especially in full-board refinement mode.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding but often lack explicit mechanisms for enforcing logical rules, leading to inconsistent outputs in structured tasks like puzzles. Neuro-symbolic AI integrates neural networks with symbolic reasoning to combine learning with formal logic. MaxSAT (Maximum Satisfiability) extends SAT by allowing soft constraints that can be violated, aiming to satisfy as many as possible while respecting hard constraints.

## [HGA Enables 8B Model Fine-Tuning to 16K Tokens on 16GB VRAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15105) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Hierarchical Global Attention (HGA) combined with segment-wise backpropagation and tiered KV storage, enabling fine-tuning of an 8B-parameter model on a 16GB GPU up to 16,384 tokens, far exceeding the 2,048-token limit of dense attention. This breakthrough dramatically lowers the hardware barrier for long-context fine-tuning, allowing researchers with consumer-grade GPUs to work with much longer sequences, which is critical for tasks like document analysis and code understanding. The method uses 4-bit QLoRA on Qwen3-8B and achieves 15.28 GB peak VRAM at 16,384 tokens, while the same adapter can evaluate up to 131,072 tokens on the same card. At 2K training length, HGA-trained adapters achieve comparable perplexity (2.7405 vs 2.7383 nat) and slightly higher throughput (217.75 vs 207.02 tokens/s) compared to dense attention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) with long contexts is memory-intensive because standard dense attention requires storing all key-value (KV) pairs in VRAM. Parameter-efficient methods like QLoRA reduce optimizer and model memory, but attention memory still scales quadratically with sequence length. HGA addresses this by only keeping the active segment differentiable in VRAM, offloading older KV to RAM or NVMe, and loading a bounded set of historical tokens for each query block.

## [Study Questions IRT Reliability for AI Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15190) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper systematically investigates how mismatches between AI benchmark data and traditional Item Response Theory (IRT) assumptions affect the reliability of IRT-based AI evaluation, using simulations from six LLM benchmarks and comparing four estimation methods across 18,000 conditions. This work is critical because IRT is increasingly used to rank AI systems and diagnose benchmark quality, but its assumptions were designed for human testing; if unreliable, it could distort model capability claims and misguide research directions. The study found that classical estimators like marginal maximum likelihood become infeasible in large benchmark settings, while scalable estimators like variational inference and neural pseudo-Siamese networks can produce unreliable item-level and ranking inferences with small or non-normally distributed model sets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Item Response Theory (IRT) is a family of statistical models originally developed in psychometrics to model the relationship between a person's latent ability and their responses to test items. It is now being applied to AI benchmarks to estimate model capabilities and rank systems. However, AI benchmark data often differs from human testing data in key ways: fewer models, many more items, and non-normal ability distributions, which can violate IRT assumptions.

A new arXiv paper proposes classical and quantum online algorithms for Markov Decision Processes that achieve regret bounds with only polylog T dependence, breaking the classical O(√T) barrier. The algorithms use a hybrid online-offline approach with a generative model to directly compute optimal policies. This work significantly advances reinforcement learning by showing that quantum computing can exponentially improve regret bounds, potentially enabling much faster learning in complex environments. It also introduces a new paradigm that avoids traditional RL techniques like optimism in the face of uncertainty. The quantum algorithms achieve regret bounds with only polylog T dependence in both finite-horizon and infinite-horizon discounted settings, while improving dependence on state space size S and action space size A. The infinite-horizon discounted regret bound is entirely new.

## [Latent Fusion Jailbreak Bypasses LLM Safety via Hidden State Interpolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10029) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Latent Fusion Jailbreak (LFJ), a white-box attack that pairs a harmful query with a structurally similar benign query and interpolates their hidden states at selected layers and token positions to elicit unsafe outputs from safety-aligned LLMs. The attack achieves a macro-averaged attack success rate of 94.13% across four safety benchmarks and five open-weight models. LFJ demonstrates that even safety-aligned LLMs remain vulnerable to representation-level attacks, highlighting a critical gap in current safety mechanisms. This work pushes the AI safety community to develop more robust defenses that go beyond input filtering and prompt-based safeguards. The attack uses refusal-loss gradients to determine intervention layers and optimizes layer-wise mixing coefficients with token-normalized compliance and refusal-suppression objectives. Dropping rejection sampling reduces ASR to 86.72%, while random pairing drops it to 27.45%, showing the importance of structured pairing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: White-box attacks assume the attacker has full access to the model's architecture and parameters, allowing manipulation of internal representations. Representation engineering is a field that studies how to interpret and control model behavior by editing hidden states. LFJ builds on these concepts to bypass safety alignment without relying on adversarial prompts.

## [HGNNs for Node Classification: Causal Analysis Questions Effectiveness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05750) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically reproduces 20 baselines across 21 datasets and introduces a causal mediation analysis framework to disentangle performance gains of heterogeneous graph neural networks (HGNNs) for node classification. It challenges the common assumption that HGNNs are inherently effective for node classification, revealing that model architecture and complexity have no causal effect, while heterogeneous information improves performance primarily through increasing homophily and local-global distribution discrepancy. The causal mediation framework treats heterogeneous relation information as the treatment, candidate structural properties as mediators, and node classification performance as the outcome, decomposing total effect into mediated and direct effects.

## [Moral Bias in LLMs Localized and Mitigated via Layer Patching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12229) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers demonstrate that the Knobe effect bias in finetuned LLMs can be localized to specific layers and eliminated by patching pretrained activations, enabling targeted bias mitigation without retraining. This work provides a novel, efficient method for mitigating social biases in LLMs without costly retraining, advancing the interpretability and safety of AI systems. The study used Layer-Patching analysis across three open-weights LLMs and found that patching activations from the pretrained model into just a few critical layers sufficed to eliminate the Knobe effect.

**Background**: The Knobe effect is a moral bias where people's judgments of intentionality are influenced by the moral valence of an action's side effects. Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks to understand their internal algorithms. Layer patching is a technique that replaces activations in specific layers of a finetuned model with those from a pretrained model to isolate and modify learned behaviors.

Researchers propose NeuroSymLand, a neuro-symbolic framework that combines probabilistic semantic scene graphs with LLM-synthesized safety rules for interpretable UAV landing site assessment. In 72 simulated and hardware-in-the-loop scenarios, it achieved 61 successful assessments, outperforming four baselines (37–57 successes). This work addresses critical limitations of learning-based UAV landing assessment—degradation under covariate shift and lack of transparency—by providing interpretable, white-box reasoning. It demonstrates that neuro-symbolic AI can be deployed on edge devices with negligible overhead, advancing safe autonomous systems. NeuroSymLand uses a lightweight segmentation model to incrementally build a probabilistic semantic scene graph encoding objects, attributes, and spatial relations. Safety rules are synthesized offline via LLMs with human-in-the-loop refinement and executed directly over the world model at runtime.

Covariate shift occurs when the distribution of input data changes between training and deployment, causing performance degradation in learning-based models.

## [DiffuMamba: Linear-Time Diffusion LMs with Mamba Backbone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.15927) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DiffuMamba replaces the Transformer backbone in masked diffusion language models with a bidirectional Mamba architecture, achieving up to 8.2x higher inference throughput on long sequences while matching downstream performance. This work addresses the key efficiency bottleneck of diffusion language models—quadratic attention or KV-cache overhead—by leveraging Mamba's linear-time sequence modeling, making diffusion-based text generation more practical for long-context applications. The authors propose DiffuMamba (pure Mamba backbone) and DiffuMamba-H (hybrid with interleaved attention), scaling up to 1.3B parameters. Cache-efficient block diffusion with Mamba mixers is identified as the only strategy that scales linearly with sequence length and achieves the strongest performance across baselines.

**Background**: Diffusion language models generate text by gradually denoising a masked or corrupted sequence, offering an alternative to autoregressive models. However, existing DLMs typically use Transformer backbones, which incur quadratic attention cost or KV-cache memory overhead during inference. Mamba is a state-space model that achieves linear-time sequence modeling with data-dependent selection, making it a promising backbone for efficient diffusion.

## [GenDistill: Fixing Distillation Evaluation for Hybrid Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26556) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that log-likelihood evaluation overestimates the quality of distilled hybrid sequence models compared to autoregressive generation, and proposes GenDistill, a multi-stage distillation pipeline that bridges this gap. This work is significant because it exposes a critical flaw in common distillation evaluation practices, which could mislead research directions and waste resources. The proposed GenDistill pipeline provides a more reliable evaluation method and achieves up to 75% KV cache reduction with 86-90% teacher accuracy retention. The paper shows that a 7B distilled model matching its teacher within 0.2 percentage points under log-likelihood scoring falls behind by 20.8 percentage points when evaluated autoregressively. The GenDistill pipeline uses a Hybrid-KDA student architecture and systematically ablates six design axes to optimize generation quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Distillation is a technique to compress large pretrained models into smaller, more efficient ones. Hybrid sequence models combine attention mechanisms with linear-time alternatives like Kimi Delta Attention (KDA) to reduce inference costs. Log-likelihood scoring evaluates models by ranking candidate answers based on probability, while autoregressive generation requires the model to produce answers token by token, which is more realistic for deployment.

## [Small Transformers Commit Early: The Prolepsis Pattern](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15010) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce prolepsis, a structural motif where small transformers commit to a decision at early layers and sustain it via task-specific attention heads without correction, even when wrong. The study replicates and extends prior planning-site findings on open models like Gemma 2 2B and Llama 3.2 1B. This work advances mechanistic interpretability by revealing how transformers make early irrevocable decisions, which has implications for AI alignment and model debugging. Understanding prolepsis could help design more flexible and corrigible models. The study uses cross-layer transcoders and sparse dictionary learning to expose planning circuits invisible to six residual-stream methods; only CLT-based steering succeeded. Prolepsis recurs across tasks but with different routing substrates, and factual recall shows the same motif at a different depth with zero overlap in attention heads.

## [Energy-Based Transport for Amortized Bayesian Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15407) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an energy-distance-based method for learning observation-dependent transport maps, enabling efficient amortized Bayesian inference for nonlinear inverse problems without requiring likelihood evaluations or invertible maps. This approach significantly reduces the computational cost of repeated Bayesian inference for new observations, making it practical for large-scale scientific and engineering inverse problems such as seismic imaging and porous-medium flow. The method uses energy distance as the training objective, which avoids computing Jacobian determinants and allows flexible parameterizations in high- and infinite-dimensional settings. It also constructs transport maps as the identity plus a learnable map in the Cameron-Martin space to ensure absolute continuity with the prior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian inference combines prior knowledge with observed data to update beliefs about unknown parameters. For nonlinear inverse problems, traditional methods like Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) are computationally expensive because they require solving a new inference problem for each observation. Amortized inference learns a reusable mapping from observations to posterior samples, enabling fast inference at test time. Transport maps are a class of methods that transform a reference distribution into the target posterior, but previous approaches often required invertibility and Jacobian computations.

Improved generalization could make neural solvers more practical for real-world routing and logistics applications where problem instances vary widely. GeoRouteNet augments a non-autoregressive encoder with centered node offsets and radii, learnable radial distance bases, distance-aware graph attention, explicit edge messages, and cross-layer representation mixing. ## [RESOURCE2SKILL: Turning Tutorials into Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29538) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RESOURCE2SKILL is a framework that distills multimodal human resources—such as tutorial videos, code repositories, and articles—into executable skills for software agents, organized in a hierarchical Skill Wiki. This framework addresses the underuse of multimodal resources in skill library construction, enabling agents to learn from diverse human-created content and improving performance by +11.9 percentage points over no-skill agents across seven domains. The Skill Wiki combines structured text, code, visual examples, metadata, and provenance, preserving complementary signals from different resource types. At inference time, agents retrieve and compose relevant skills, and can acquire new skills online when coverage is insufficient.

## [vLLM Configurations Significantly Impact Energy, Performance, Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09172) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A large-scale study with 9,000 runs evaluated how vLLM configurations (attention kernel, prefix caching, chunked prefill) affect energy, latency, and accuracy across 5 LLMs and 5 tasks, revealing significant trade-offs and interaction effects. This study provides crucial empirical guidance for deploying LLMs efficiently, showing that inference engine configuration tuning can yield local improvements along the Pareto frontier, while model choice dominates global trade-offs. Attention type and prefix caching had major impacts on energy and performance, while chunked prefill had limited effect under default settings. Unexpectedly, inference options also affected model accuracy.

**Background**: vLLM is a high-throughput inference engine for LLMs. Key configuration options include attention kernel type (e.g., FlashAttention, Triton), prefix caching (reusing KV cache for common prefixes), and chunked prefill (splitting large prefills to batch with decodes). These options can affect performance and energy but their combined effects were poorly understood.

## [GNN-Surrogate Metriplectic Twin for Brain Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10439) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a graph neural network (GNN) surrogate metriplectic twin that models the human motor cortex as a port-Hamiltonian system, capturing non-equilibrium steady states and enabling closed-loop neuromodulation. This work bridges port-Hamiltonian theory, GNNs, and EEG modeling, offering a physically principled framework for brain-computer interfaces and neuromodulation, with potential to improve understanding of cortical dynamics and closed-loop therapies. The model uses a skew-symmetric coupling for conservative interconnections and a GNN surrogate for state-dependent dissipation, with a phase-locking prior gating the functional connectome. It reproduces near-critical avalanche branching but fails to match the aperiodic 1/f slope and long-range temporal correlations of real cortex.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Port-Hamiltonian systems extend classical Hamiltonian mechanics to include energy exchange with the environment via ports, making them suitable for modeling open physical systems. Metriplectic formulations combine conservative (symplectic) and dissipative (metric) dynamics, ideal for non-equilibrium thermodynamics. Graph neural networks serve as efficient surrogates for complex simulations on graph-structured data.

## [Scaling Point-in-Time LMs Narrows Lookahead Bias Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11889) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers trained decoder-only transformers up to 4B parameters on 1T temporally filtered tokens from FineWeb, creating monthly point-in-time language model checkpoints from 2013 to 2024. These models nearly match the performance of unconstrained models like Gemma-3-4B and LLaMA-7B on common benchmarks. This work addresses lookahead bias in LLMs, a critical issue for valid backtesting and causal inference in finance and social sciences. By showing that scaling can nearly close the performance gap, it makes point-in-time models practical for real-world applications. The models are decoder-only transformers with up to 4B parameters, trained on 1T tokens from FineWeb with chronological filtering. Instruction fine-tuning via LoRA further improves downstream usability, and the complete pipeline is released for reproducibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models trained on unrestricted internet data can embed future information, causing lookahead bias that invalidates backtests and causal inference. Point-in-time language models avoid this by training only on data available up to each date, but previous efforts suffered from significant performance degradation. This work shows that scaling up model size and training data can substantially close that gap.

Researchers propose Code-MUE, a black-box framework that measures uncertainty in code LLMs by constructing execution-based semantic interaction graphs and computing Von Neumann entropy of the solution space. This addresses a critical gap in uncertainty estimation for code LLMs, enabling reliable risk detection and selective prediction in software engineering without requiring access to model internals. Code-MUE achieves a strong negative correlation with functional correctness (Spearman's correlation up to -0.98), significantly outperforming lexical and embedding-based baselines across eight state-of-the-art LLMs.

**Background**: Code LLMs are increasingly used in software engineering but their stochastic outputs can introduce critical errors. Existing uncertainty estimation methods either require internal model access (white/grey-box) or rely on superficial text similarity, which fails for code where syntax and semantics can diverge. Code-MUE bridges this syntax-semantics gap by grounding uncertainty in observable runtime behavior.

This challenges the widespread practice of using LLMs for iterative refinement in AI pipelines, highlighting that reference-free judge signals are too weak and unstable for closed-loop optimization, with implications for any system relying on LLM feedback for self-correction. ## [MxGPS: Multiplex Graph Transformer for Power Grid Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13763) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose MxGPS, a multiplex graph transformer that uses multi-task learning and self-supervised pre-training to mitigate topology overfitting in power grid GNNs, achieving 0% boundary violation rate on unseen topologies. This work identifies and addresses a critical failure mode in power grid GNNs—topology overfitting—where models perform well on training grids but fail on unseen ones. MxGPS demonstrates that multi-task joint training enables topology-agnostic generalization with far fewer parameters, advancing foundation models for power systems. MxGPS runs K task-specialized GPS branches over a shared node encoder, jointly trained on Static State Estimation and AC Power Flow via self-supervised pre-training and multi-task fine-tuning. With only 1.6M parameters (12x fewer than GridFM), it degrades only 39% under topology shift, while single-task models degrade 190% to 1400%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are widely used for power grid analysis but often fail when applied to unseen grid topologies. The GPS (General, Powerful, Scalable) graph transformer combines local message-passing with global attention for efficient graph learning. Topology overfitting refers to the tendency of task-specific gradients to encode training topology patterns rather than underlying physics, causing poor generalization.

## [Generative Compilation: Real-Time Compiler Feedback for AI Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13921) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced generative compilation, a method that provides real-time compiler feedback during AI code generation by transforming partial programs into complete ones using a lightweight transformation called a sealor. This approach addresses a key challenge in generating correct code for strict languages like Rust, reducing non-compiling outputs and improving functional correctness compared to post-generation feedback alone. The sealor is a syntax-guided transformation that ensures possible-to-complete partial programs are never rejected while preserving enough context to catch genuine dead ends early; it was mechanized in Lean for a core Rust-like calculus and extended to real Rust.

Generative compilation bridges this gap by enabling compiler feedback during generation.

## [NexForge: Requirement-Driven Task Synthesis for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14186) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NexForge is a requirement-driven framework that synthesizes diverse, executable agent tasks and expert trajectories for supervised fine-tuning (SFT) from high-level capability requirements, overcoming the limitations of substrate-bound task synthesis. It generated 3,600 terminal tasks and 2,000 office tasks without domain-specific infrastructure, improving Qwen3.5-35B-A3B Base from 22.5% to 52.0% on Terminal-Bench 2.0 and from 813 to 1338 Elo on GDPval. This approach reduces manual engineering and biases in post-training data generation, enabling scalable and domain-agnostic agent training. It achieved state-of-the-art open-source performance with Nex-N2 models, surpassing several proprietary systems, which could accelerate the development of capable LLM agents across diverse real-world domains. NexForge first profiles real-world demand into representative scenarios and task profiles, then performs distribution-aware compilation to automatically retrieve or construct files, repositories, dependencies, and runtime configurations. Scaling to 43.2K terminal tasks further improved performance to 58.4%, surpassing Claude Opus 4.6, and the Nex-N2 models reached 75.3% on Terminal-Bench 2.1 and 1585 Elo on GDPval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: LLM post-training often relies on supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with expert trajectories, but traditional task synthesis is substrate-bound—tasks are generated from fixed tools, repositories, or skill graphs, requiring manual engineering for new domains and inheriting biases. NexForge addresses this by starting from high-level capability requirements, automatically compiling executable tasks and expert rollouts, thus enabling scalable and unbiased data generation.

The article challenges the assumption that SSAO's corner darkening is realistic, using empirical evidence.

Firefox has been working on Vulkan Video support for some time, and this merge brings it to the mainstream browser.

## [GraphDx: Cost-Aware Multi-Agent Framework for Sequential Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15280) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GraphDx introduces a novel multi-agent framework that uses LLMs to automatically construct Medical Diagnosis Knowledge Graphs (MDKGs) with cost-aware attributes, and employs three collaborative agents (Perception, Reasoning, Decision) to perform sequential diagnosis. Experiments on MedQA and MIMIC-IV show diagnostic success rates improve from 50–68% to 79–93% while reducing test costs by 20–54%. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM-based diagnosis: the inability to reason under cost constraints, which often leads to excessive testing. By combining knowledge graphs with multi-agent orchestration, GraphDx offers a more economical and interpretable solution for automated clinical diagnosis, potentially reducing healthcare costs and improving diagnostic accuracy. The MDKG features quantized typicality, action-centric topology, and dual-objective attributes for diagnostic relevance and cost-sensitivity. The Reasoning Agent performs deterministic evidence scoring and cost-aware planning on the MDKG, while Perception and Decision Agents handle language understanding and generation. The framework was evaluated on three LLM backbones: DeepSeek-V3, Kimi-k2, and Llama-3.3.

**Background**: Sequential diagnosis involves iteratively gathering information (e.g., tests) to balance diagnostic accuracy and resource costs. Existing LLMs encode extensive medical knowledge but struggle with systematic reasoning under cost constraints, often ordering unnecessary tests. Knowledge graphs provide structured medical knowledge, and multi-agent frameworks can decompose complex tasks into specialized roles. GraphDx combines these ideas to create a cost-aware diagnostic system.

## [Transparent Interface Helps Users Escape Filter Bubbles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15284) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A study published on arXiv (2607.15284) designed a transparent news recommendation interface that shows users the political and topical interests inferred by the system, allowing them to adjust recommendations. In a user study, this approach helped most users recognize they were in a filter bubble and reduced extreme content consumption. Filter bubbles are a major concern in personalized news consumption, as they can reinforce biases and polarize society. This research demonstrates that giving users transparency and control can mitigate filter bubbles, though it also shows heterogeneous effects, highlighting the need for careful design. The enhanced interface exposed inferred political stance and topic interests, enabling users to request more articles on a specific topic or political leaning. While most users moved toward the center, some used the control to seek more extreme content, and overall political diversity decreased.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: A filter bubble is an online environment where algorithms only expose users to information that aligns with their existing beliefs, isolating them from diverse perspectives. Traditional recommendation systems often operate as black boxes, leaving users unaware of how their preferences are being shaped. This study explores a transparent, scrutable system that lets users see and adjust the factors driving their recommendations.

<a id="item-132"></a>
## [Error Structure of Hadamard vs FFT for Convolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15293) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper characterizes the algebraic error when using the Hadamard transform instead of the FFT for circular-dyadic convolution, revealing exact error cancellation at two input and two output positions, a nearly full-rank error operator, and asymptotic doubling of output energy for most filters. This work provides a theoretical foundation for understanding and predicting the error when substituting the Hadamard transform for the FFT in convolution, which could enable more efficient signal processing by leveraging the Hadamard transform's real-valued sign flips and lower computational cost. The error operator's null space has only logarithmic dimension, meaning the error is almost always present. The expected error is governed by a single alignment scalar, with a closed-form expression derived by averaging over random filters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Circular convolution is typically computed efficiently using the FFT via the convolution theorem. The Hadamard transform, which uses only real arithmetic and sign flips, diagonalizes dyadic convolution (based on XOR indexing) instead of circular convolution. Substituting the Hadamard transform for the DFT changes the operation being computed, introducing algebraic error that this paper characterizes.

AV-JEPA extends LeJEPA to audio-visual self-supervised learning using an early-fusion Vision Transformer and modality dropout as masking, achieving competitive classification on VGGSound (57.1% top-1) and AudioSet (32.7 mAP) and zero-shot audio-video retrieval. This work simplifies multimodal self-supervised learning by removing decoders, EMA teachers, and contrastive losses, offering a cleaner architecture that could inspire more efficient and scalable multimodal models. AV-JEPA uses the SIGReg objective to align embeddings with an isotropic Gaussian distribution, and modality dropout randomly masks entire modalities during training to encourage cross-modal alignment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: LeJEPA is a self-supervised learning framework that uses predictive embeddings and isotropic Gaussian regularization (SIGReg) to create well-structured latent representations. Modality dropout is a technique that randomly omits entire modalities during training to improve robustness to missing data. AV-JEPA combines these ideas for audio-visual learning.

Existing methods use a probability-sampled audit set with human labels to correct these errors, but often assume human labels are perfect. In practice, human labels can be noisy, and only some audited items are reviewed by an expert (adjudicated). PA-DSL leverages the adjudicated subset to improve the audit labels and then debias the automated labels.

Researchers introduced SLAPBench, the first benchmark for evaluating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on four-finger SLAP fingerprint verification, built from NIST SD302b with 7,832 pairs. They found that prompting strategies critically affect model performance, with task-description prompts causing collapse in open-source models, while Claude Opus 4.8 resisted collapse and achieved the best binary result (FAR = 20.2%). This work establishes the first baseline for MLLM-based SLAP fingerprint verification, a task relevant to border control and law enforcement. It highlights that prompting governs collapse while model capability governs discrimination, providing guidance for deploying MLLMs in high-stakes biometric applications. The benchmark evaluates four open-source MLLMs (InternVL3-8B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, Qwen3-VL-8B, Gemma-3-12B) and Claude Opus 4.8 under zero-shot, task-description, and similarity-scoring prompts. Under similarity scoring, Qwen3-VL-8B achieved perfect AUC of 1.000, but the authors treat this as a diagnostic for near-duplicate detection rather than genuine capability, as mated pairs in SD302b are single captures rendered twice.

## [KANs Tested as MLP Replacements in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15525) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper empirically evaluates Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) as feed-forward replacements in small language models, finding that while they offer interpretability and competitive performance, their benefits are tied to low-capacity settings and do not universally outperform MLPs. This study provides crucial empirical evidence on the practical utility of KANs, a promising alternative to MLPs, but cautions that their advantages may not scale to larger models or standard benchmarks, guiding future research directions. In a 10M-parameter B-spline KAN, 87.8% of edges exceeded a nonlinearity threshold and only 0.4% were inactive; pruning 20-25% of low-activity edges caused negligible loss increase. However, near-total fPCA compression and high closed-form-fit coverage were properties of the low-capacity grid-2 basis, not universal KAN behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) are a neural network architecture inspired by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem, replacing fixed activation functions with learned univariate functions, often using B-splines. They have been proposed as a more interpretable and potentially more efficient alternative to traditional multilayer perceptrons (MLPs). This paper tests KANs as replacements for feed-forward layers in small transformer-based language models.

This paper proposes modeling address in multi-party dialogue as continuous levels rather than discrete labels, using a latent-variable model to infer continuous address levels from annotator judgments. This challenges the common assumption that address is discrete, and shows that continuous address levels better predict turn-taking and listener behaviors like gaze and backchannels, potentially improving dialogue system design. The study uses a multi-party human dialogue corpus with multiple annotators, constructing both binary labels via majority vote and continuous levels via a latent-variable model, then comparing their predictive fit for turn-taking, gaze, and backchannels.

Researchers propose DDR-SR, a difficulty-aware dynamic routing framework for diffusion-based real-world image super-resolution that adaptively assigns inputs to experts of varying capacity based on restoration difficulty. This addresses the one-size-fits-all limitation of existing diffusion super-resolution methods, improving efficiency for easy images and preserving fine details for hard ones, which is crucial for practical applications. The framework uses a high-frequency energy-based difficulty estimator and two experts: Expert-D4 (4x VAE downsampling) for hard samples and Expert-D8 (8x downsampling) for easy ones, balancing fidelity and efficiency.

## [drXAI: XAI-Driven Data Reduction for Scalable Time Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15774) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced drXAI, a method that repurposes XAI attribution scores to reduce data in time series classification, achieving 80-90% data reduction while maintaining accuracy. It uses a fast GPU-accelerated Hydra classifier to generate attributions and an automated elbow-cut heuristic for feature selection. This work demonstrates that XAI can provide measurable performance gains beyond interpretability, directly addressing scalability bottlenecks in modern time series classifiers like Transformers. It enables resource-intensive models to handle previously inaccessible large datasets, benefiting applications in finance, healthcare, and IoT. drXAI aggregates local attributions from Hydra into global feature importance scores and uses an automated elbow-cut heuristic to select salient features without manual thresholds. Evaluated on synthetic and real-world univariate and multivariate datasets, it recovers ground-truth features where traditional baselines fail and allows ConvTran to scale to previously memory-prohibitive datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Time series classification (TSC) is crucial for many domains, but state-of-the-art models like Transformers have quadratic complexity in sequence length and linear complexity in channels, making them computationally expensive for large datasets. Explainable AI (XAI) attribution methods highlight which input features most influence a model's prediction, but have primarily been used for interpretability rather than performance improvement. Hydra is a fast, GPU-accelerated convolutional kernel-based classifier that serves as an efficient surrogate for generating attributions in drXAI.

## [TANGO: Test-Time Noise Guidance for Better Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15849) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TANGO (Terminal points Avoidance through Noise Guided Optimization), a test-time adaptation method that enforces noise distribution consistency to reduce error accumulation in autoregressive video diffusion models. This addresses a critical limitation of autoregressive video generation—error accumulation over long sequences—and achieves a 3.1% absolute improvement on VBench and a 28.3% reduction in Fréchet Video Distance, making long video generation more practical. TANGO uses the diffusion model itself as a critic by predicting one step forward and requiring isotropic Gaussian noise prediction; it searches for alternative trajectories when deviation is detected. The method is evaluated on 15-second videos and code is open-sourced.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive video diffusion models generate long videos by conditioning only on past frames, improving efficiency but suffering from error accumulation as generated frames drift from the training distribution. Existing methods anchor frames to the real manifold but still encounter terminal points where the model cannot continue. TANGO enforces that the predicted noise distribution matches the forward noising process, preventing the model from reaching such terminal points.

Researchers propose ToxGate, a trust-fusion mechanism that conditions external toxicity signals on encoder representations to improve abuse detection in multilingual and code-mixed text. The method achieves gains in 10 of 12 in-domain and 7 of 8 transfer settings, with largest improvements in high-risk slices like explicit slurs and violent threats. This work addresses a critical gap in content moderation: existing toxicity tools are unreliable for code-mixed and multilingual text common in Indian languages. By treating external signals as conditional evidence rather than fixed features, ToxGate offers a principled way to improve moderation accuracy in high-risk scenarios. ToxGate is evaluated across three short-text abuse datasets, four transformer encoders, and five seeds per setting. Source-specific gating yields the strongest results in transfer, severe-abuse slices, and high-risk triage.

## [QEWC: Quantum Fisher Info Mitigates Forgetting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16030) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Quantum Elastic Weight Consolidation (QEWC), a regularization method that uses quantum Fisher information (QFI) to identify important parameters and mitigate catastrophic forgetting in variational quantum classifiers trained on sequential tasks. This work addresses a critical challenge in quantum continual learning, enabling quantum models to retain knowledge across tasks without forgetting, which is essential for practical quantum machine learning applications. QEWC uses QFI to measure the intrinsic sensitivity of the parameterized quantum state, providing a denser state-geometric constraint compared to classical Fisher information (CFI)-based EWC. Under depolarizing noise, QFI preserves a more stable sensitivity structure than CFI, which is strongly suppressed by degraded measurement statistics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Catastrophic forgetting is a phenomenon where neural networks lose previously learned knowledge when trained on new tasks. Elastic Weight Consolidation (EWC) is a classical method that uses Fisher information to protect important weights. Quantum Fisher information extends this concept to quantum systems, measuring the sensitivity of quantum states to parameter changes.

It bridges the gap between high-level governance principles and metric-driven approaches, providing a practical, auditable way to monitor and reassess AI trustworthiness over time, which is crucial for compliance with emerging AI regulations. The methodology introduces concepts like trustworthiness plateaus, level transitions, boundary margins, and profile drift, and embeds them in a conformity-oriented workflow with human responsibilities and control gates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: AI governance requires ongoing assessment of whether AI systems remain trustworthy over time, but existing approaches are either too abstract for lifecycle monitoring or too narrowly metric-focused to connect with governance needs.
