## [ToolSciVer: Tool-Augmented Framework for Scientific Claim Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16131) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ToolSciVer is a novel framework that augments vision-language models with three type-aware visual tools—table row/column focus, chart-to-structure parsing, and high-resolution region zoom—and trains the policy using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with a composite reward. It achieves superior performance on SciVer and MuSciClaims benchmarks across five VLMs from three model families. This work addresses the challenging problem of multimodal scientific claim verification, where models must locate and interpret visual evidence from figures, tables, and charts. By introducing tool-augmented reinforcement learning, it significantly improves the reliability of automated verification of scientific claims, which has implications for research integrity and literature review. ToolSciVer uses three specialized tools: table row/column focus for extracting specific cells, chart-to-structure parsing for converting charts into structured data, and high-resolution region zoom for examining fine details. The GRPO-based training optimizes a composite reward that includes answer correctness, format validity, length control, tool-use efficiency, and tool-validity penalties.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Scientific Claim Verification (MSCV) requires models to verify claims using evidence from figures, tables, charts, and text in scientific papers. Existing methods often fail to locate decisive visual evidence or accurately read structured visuals. ToolSciVer is the first tool-augmented framework for MSCV, leveraging reinforcement learning to teach models when and how to use visual tools.

## [Muon Optimizer Boosts Agentic RL by 88%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16169) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study shows that the Muon optimizer, when applied to hidden weight matrices in Group-in-Group Policy Optimization (GiGPO), improves final-window validation success on ALFWorld from 0.290 to 0.546, an 88% increase over AdamW. This finding suggests that Muon can significantly enhance agentic reinforcement learning, a critical area for training LLM agents, and challenges the dominance of AdamW in RL post-training. The study uses single-seed comparisons on ALFWorld with Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct and GiGPO, and finds that Muon's benefit depends on the advantage estimator and learning rate; at 1e-5, Muon reaches 0.901 success rate and accelerates convergence by 30-60 updates.

**Background**: Muon is an optimizer designed for 2D parameters that uses Newton-Schulz iteration to orthogonalize SGD-momentum updates, achieving state-of-the-art results in pre-training. GiGPO is a group-based RL algorithm for LLM agents that provides fine-grained credit assignment without a critic. ALFWorld is a text-based embodied AI benchmark for household tasks.

## [Open-Weight LLMs Evaluated for CAV Threat Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16175) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers evaluated 11 open-weight LLMs (4B to 120B parameters) for generating structured threat information (STIX) from CAV vulnerability descriptions, introducing the CAV-STIXGen dataset. Single-model configurations achieved F1 scores of 0.94 for SDO, 0.63 for SRO, and 0.99 for CWE mapping. This work addresses a critical need for automated threat intelligence in autonomous vehicle security, enabling faster and more consistent vulnerability management. It demonstrates that open-weight LLMs can effectively transform unstructured CVE descriptions into structured STIX formats, potentially improving defense prioritization. The CAV-STIXGen dataset maps CAV vulnerability descriptions to STIX domain objects (SDO), STIX relationship objects (SRO), CWE, and MITRE ATT&CK techniques. Complete MITRE ATT&CK mapping remains challenging, and multi-agent setups showed lower F1 scores (0.91 for SDOs, 0.43 for SROs).

## [RAD: Retrieval High-quality Demonstrations for Offline RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15356) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RAD introduces a retrieval mechanism that selects high-return, reachable states from an offline dataset as target waypoints, then uses a generative model to plan sub-trajectories toward those targets, improving policy generalization in offline reinforcement learning. 该方法通过利用现有高质量示范而非合成数据，解决了离线强化学习的一个关键局限——在训练分布之外泛化能力差，有望在机器人、自动驾驶等实际应用中实现更安全、更有效的部署。 RAD retrieves states based on both return and reachability, and conditions a diffusion model on the target state to generate sub-trajectories. Experiments show competitive or superior performance across diverse offline RL benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning learns policies from fixed datasets without environment interaction, but struggles to generalize to unseen scenarios. Prior methods often use synthetic data augmentation, which can fail to cover novel situations. RAD instead retrieves high-return states from the dataset and uses them as waypoints for diffusion-based planning, inspired by recent work on stitching sub-trajectories with conditional diffusion models.

## [Neuro-Symbolic Framework Integrates GNNs with Relational Bayesian Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21873) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a neuro-symbolic framework that integrates graph neural networks (GNNs) into Relational Bayesian Networks (RBNs) for enhanced probabilistic reasoning on graph data. Two implementations are developed: one compiles GNNs directly into the native RBN language, and the other maintains the GNN as an external component. This work bridges the gap between GNNs' predictive power and RBNs' reasoning capabilities, enabling novel applications such as collective classification and multi-objective network optimization. It represents a significant step toward more robust and interpretable AI systems that combine learning and reasoning. The framework includes a maximum a posteriori (MAP) inference method for the neuro-symbolic models. The paper demonstrates the approach on two distinct problems: collective node classification with explicit modeling of homo- and heterophily, and a multi-objective network optimization problem in environmental planning, both with new benchmark datasets.

Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks and symbolic reasoning to leverage the strengths of both paradigms.

This paper investigates fairness and explainability in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for wellbeing assessment, revealing significant performance and bias variations across datasets and architectures. It proposes an XAI intervention framework that achieves mixed results, including perfect equal opportunity for Qwen2-VL at a severe accuracy cost on E-DAIC. This work addresses the under-explored intersection of fairness, XAI, and VLMs in mental health assessment, highlighting persistent gaps between procedural transparency and equitable outcomes. It provides concrete recommendations for future fairness interventions that must jointly optimize accuracy, demographic parity, and cross-domain generalization. Phi3.5-Vision achieved 80.4% accuracy on E-DAIC while Qwen2-VL struggled at 33.9%; both models over-predicted depression on AFAR-BSFT. Qwen2-VL showed higher gender disparities, while Phi-3.5-Vision exhibited more racial bias; explainability-based interventions improved procedural consistency but sometimes amplified racial bias.

## [GA-VINO: Geometry-Aware Neural Operator for Plate Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16624) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose GA-VINO, a geometry-aware variational physics-informed neural operator that predicts Mindlin-Reissner plate responses under varying geometries, materials, and loads using boundary point clouds and multi-branch encoding. This method enables rapid full-field prediction for plate structures without labeled data, which could significantly accelerate engineering design and optimization in fields like aerospace and civil engineering. GA-VINO uses a variational physics-informed loss based on the discretized total potential energy of the Mindlin-Reissner plate, avoiding the need for labeled solution data during training. It processes irregular point clouds directly, allowing different physical fields to be discretized on different point sets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Mindlin-Reissner plate theory extends Kirchhoff-Love theory by accounting for shear deformations, making it suitable for thick plates. Traditional numerical methods like finite element analysis require repeated modeling when geometry or loads change, which is time-consuming. Physics-informed neural operators aim to learn the mapping from parameters to solutions without labeled data by incorporating physical laws into the loss function.

## [UCOB: Credit-Aware Self-Distillation for Agentic Skill Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29502) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UCOB introduces a credit-aware on-policy bidirectional self-distillation framework that treats skill-conditioned and no-skill prompts as two context views, using the higher-return view as a local teacher to improve agentic reinforcement learning. This work addresses the fragile privileged-teacher assumption in skill-based RL, enabling agents to dynamically utilize and evolve skills without relying on a fixed oracle, which can significantly improve performance in complex agentic tasks like ALFWorld and WebShop. UCOB outperforms skill-free RL, skill-memory baselines, and self-distillation methods across model scales, achieving up to 23.5 and 18.0 point gains over SOTA baselines on ALFWorld and WebShop respectively, with modest training overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning often uses skill memories to reuse past experience as textual guidance. However, retrieved skills are not always beneficial; they may help in one state but mislead in another, making the common assumption that a skill-conditioned prompt is a fixed teacher for the no-skill prompt unreliable. UCOB's credit-aware bidirectional self-distillation overcomes this by comparing returns from both views and using the better-performing one as teacher.

## [First-Order Modal Logic Embeddings in Isabelle/HOL with Faithfulness Proof](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10880) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends deep-and-shallow embedding methodology to first-order modal logic (FML) in Isabelle/HOL, providing three embeddings (deep, maximal-shallow, minimal-shallow) and a mechanized faithfulness proof using the Löwenheim-Skolem theorem. This work advances formal verification of modal logics by enabling automated reasoning about first-order modal systems, which is crucial for applications in philosophy, computer science, and AI. The mechanized faithfulness proof ensures that shallow embeddings correctly capture deep semantics, increasing trust in formalized reasoning. The minimal-shallow embedding is presented as an Isabelle/HOL locale parameterized by accessibility relation, world-indexed interpretation, universe of worlds, and variable assignment. The Löwenheim-Skolem theorem is mechanized to resolve surjectivity issues with countable variable assignments over uncountable domains, enabling the faithfulness proof.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Deep and shallow embeddings are two techniques for formalizing a logic within a theorem prover: deep embeddings represent syntax as data structures, while shallow embeddings reuse the host logic's constructs. The Löwenheim-Skolem theorem states that any countable first-order theory with an infinite model has models of all infinite cardinalities, which is used here to handle domain size issues. Isabelle/HOL locales provide a way to parameterize theories, enabling modular reasoning.

Researchers propose VL4ML, a human-centered explainability framework that communicates AI predictions and uncertainty through intuitive visual patterns instead of numerical or post-hoc explanations. A study with 158 participants (39.2% clinical professionals) showed over 79% positive ratings, 84% found visual outputs more memorable, and 76.9% reported faster decision-making. This work addresses a critical gap in clinical AI: making uncertainty understandable to non-experts. By enabling clinicians and patients to interpret predictions without statistical training, VL4ML could improve trust and adoption of AI in healthcare. VL4ML encodes diagnostic information using colors, patterns, and spatial structures, and was validated across classification, regression, longitudinal prediction, and multimodal analysis tasks. Over 82% of participants successfully perceived uncertainty embedded in visual representations without prior statistical training, with no significant differences between clinicians and non-clinicians.

## [Training-Free LLM Alignment via Distillation and Delta Debugging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01514) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce DAPA, a training-free, plug-and-play method that uses knowledge distillation and delta debugging to correct shadow alignment in LLMs, achieving a 14.42% average improvement in defense success rate across 17 models without performance loss. This method addresses the practical issue of shadow alignment, where fine-tuning on benign tasks can inadvertently reintroduce unsafe behaviors, offering a robust safety enhancement that does not require additional training or data. The approach uses delta debugging to identify critical knowledge components for distillation, then fuses alignment signals from well-aligned models into shadow-aligned models via model fusion. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Shadow alignment refers to the phenomenon where a safely aligned LLM can be subverted by fine-tuning on a small set of benign examples, causing the model to revert to unsafe behaviors. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a teacher model to a student model, while delta debugging is a technique to isolate minimal critical components that cause a property to hold.

## [CTC: A New Benchmark for Cooperative MARL Requiring Division of Labor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00345) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The Composite Task Challenge (CTC) is a new benchmark suite for cooperative multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) that explicitly requires division of labor and cooperation for task success. The paper evaluates nine representative MARL methods, all of which achieve zero test winning rates, highlighting the difficulty of CTC tasks. CTC fills a critical gap in cooperative MARL benchmarks by explicitly requiring division of labor, which is essential for real-world applications. This benchmark will drive the development of more capable cooperative MARL methods that can handle complex, interdependent tasks. CTC tasks are built on two design principles: division of labor is necessary for success, and failure in any atomic subtask leads to overall task failure. The paper also provides a guiding solution that achieves non-zero but suboptimal winning rates, confirming the tasks are solvable yet challenging.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Cooperative multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) involves multiple agents learning to work together to achieve a common goal. Division of labor (DOL) is a key mechanism for improving cooperation in MARL, but existing benchmarks often do not explicitly require DOL, limiting progress. CTC addresses this by designing tasks where agents must specialize and coordinate to succeed.

This work addresses a key bottleneck in Anchors—its computational inefficiency—making it practical for time-sensitive applications like real-time model debugging and decision support. MAnchors maintains a memory of low-precision, high-coverage rules and uses horizontal (feature replacement) and vertical (refinement) transformations to adapt them to new inputs.

AuditVotes introduces a novel framework that integrates graph rewiring augmentation and conditional smoothing into randomized smoothing for Graph Neural Networks (GNNs), achieving high clean accuracy and strong certified robustness simultaneously. This work addresses the critical accuracy-robustness trade-off in certified robustness for GNNs, enabling practical deployment in security-sensitive domains like fraud detection and recommendation systems. On Cora-ML under 20-edge attacks, AuditVotes improves clean accuracy by 437.1% and certified accuracy by 409.3% compared to vanilla smoothing, while maintaining comparable runtime. The framework also provides theoretical guarantees that certified robustness is preserved under arbitrary filtering functions.

Randomized smoothing is a popular method to provide certified robustness, but it often suffers from a severe accuracy-robustness trade-off. Graph rewiring modifies graph topology to improve information flow, while conditional smoothing filters low-confidence predictions to enhance consistency.

A mixed-methods study evaluated a scaffolded GenAI literacy intervention called 'AI-Lab' across three CS courses and one engineering course, finding that it increased student openness and comfort with GenAI for conceptual, debugging, and homework tasks without increasing self-reported use on graded work. This study provides evidence that brief, structured instruction can foster responsible GenAI use in early undergraduate programming courses, addressing a critical need as GenAI tools become ubiquitous in computing education. This method eliminates the need for ground-truth data, which is often unavailable in medical imaging, and reduces inference to a single forward pass, making it suitable for time-critical applications like emergency CT scans. The framework leverages similarities between iterative reconstruction, Deep Image Prior, and unrolled optimization schemes, and is evaluated on the 2DeteCT dataset, achieving competitive or better results than filtered back-projection and supervised networks.

## [LaRT: Jointly Evaluating LLMs via Accuracy and CoT Length](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07019) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose the Latency-Response Theory (LaRT) model, which jointly evaluates large language models using both response accuracy and chain-of-thought length, introducing a correlation parameter between latent ability and latent speed. LaRT provides a more comprehensive evaluation of LLMs by incorporating reasoning efficiency (CoT length) alongside accuracy, potentially leading to better model rankings and more efficient testing compared to traditional Item Response Theory. LaRT uses a stochastic approximation Expectation-Maximization algorithm for parameter estimation and establishes rigorous identifiability results. Experiments show LaRT yields different LLM rankings than IRT and outperforms IRT on predictive power, item efficiency, ranking validity, and evaluation efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Item Response Theory (IRT) is a psychometric framework that models the probability of a correct response based on latent ability and item parameters. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting elicits intermediate reasoning steps from LLMs, and CoT length can indicate reasoning effort. LaRT extends IRT by jointly modeling response accuracy and CoT length as indicators of latent ability and speed.

This paper introduces low-recoverability steganography for large language models, replacing arbitrary mappings with embedding-space-derived ones, and proposes mechanistic interpretability-based detection using linear probes on later-layer activations. It highlights a new security threat where fine-tuned LLMs can covertly encode secrets, and shows that traditional steganalysis is insufficient, suggesting interpretability-based defenses as a promising direction for AI safety. Experiments on Llama-8B, Ministral-8B, and Llama-70B show exact secret recovery increases by 78-123% while reducing payload recoverability, and linear probes detect secrets with up to 33% higher accuracy in fine-tuned models.

Prior work achieved 100% recoverability, but this paper reduces recoverability by using embedding-space geometry. Traditional steganalysis detects distributional shifts, but fine-tuning inherently causes such shifts, making detection harder.

KDFlow supports both off-policy and on-policy distillation, and includes cross-tokenizer KD algorithms through extensible APIs. The decoupled architecture separates teacher inference (SGLang) from student training (FSDP2), optimizing each for its role.

## [Soft Q(λ): Multi-step Off-policy Entropy RL Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13780) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Soft Q(λ), a multi-step off-policy method for entropy-regularized reinforcement learning that combines a novel Soft Tree Backup operator with eligibility traces to enable efficient credit assignment under arbitrary behavior policies. Soft Q(λ) fills a gap in entropy-regularized RL by extending soft Q-learning to fully off-policy, multi-step scenarios, which can improve sample efficiency and learning stability in complex tasks. The method formalizes an n-step formulation for soft Q-learning and introduces the Soft Tree Backup operator to handle off-policy updates, then unifies these into an eligibility trace framework called Soft Q(λ).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Soft Q-learning is a model-free method for entropy-regularized reinforcement learning that optimizes returns with a penalty on divergence from a reference policy. Eligibility traces are a technique that allows multi-step credit assignment by maintaining a temporary record of state-action visits, improving upon one-step TD methods.

## [Brain-CLIPLM: Semantic Compression for EEG-to-Text Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16370) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Brain-CLIPLM, a two-stage framework that first recovers semantic anchors from EEG via contrastive learning, then reconstructs sentences using a retrieval-grounded large language model with chain-of-thought prompting. This work introduces a semantic compression hypothesis that reframes EEG-to-text decoding as recovering compressed semantic content rather than full sentences, potentially improving decoding accuracy and robustness under realistic information constraints. On the ZuCo benchmark, Brain-CLIPLM achieves 67.6% Top-5 and 85.0% Top-25 sentence retrieval accuracy, with strongest performance at intermediate anchor granularity. Control analyses confirm that EEG-derived anchors carry sentence-specific information beyond language-model priors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Decoding natural language from non-invasive EEG is challenging due to low signal-to-noise ratio and limited information bandwidth. Traditional approaches attempt direct sentence reconstruction, which may be overly fine-grained relative to the recoverable information scale. The semantic compression hypothesis posits that EEG preserves recoverable semantic anchors rather than full lexical-syntactic form.

## [memorywire: A Vendor-Neutral Wire Format for Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01138) ⭐️ 7.0/10

memorywire proposes a standardized JSON-Schema 2020-12 wire format for five agent memory operations (remember, recall, forget, merge, expire) across four memory types (semantic, episodic, procedural, emotional), along with a reference implementation and benchmarks. This addresses the fragmentation in agent memory frameworks by enabling interoperability, reducing integration effort, and introducing a governance channel for human oversight, which is critical for trustworthy AI systems. The reference implementation includes five backend adapters (sqlite-vec, mem0, Letta, Cognee, pgvector) and achieves recall@5=1.000 on a 100-fact corpus with ingest p50=37.8ms and recall p50=40.6ms. It also passes 68 of 80 cross-adapter conformance tests with zero failures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Agent memory frameworks like mem0, Letta, and Cognee each have proprietary APIs and storage formats, making integration and migration costly. memorywire standardizes the communication protocol between agents and memory stores, similar to how SQL standardizes database queries. It also includes a human-in-the-loop (HITL) governance channel for reviewing memory writes before storage.

## [UMAP's kNN Graph Unlocks New Data Sensemaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08746) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper shows that applying standard graph algorithms like PageRank, k-core decomposition, and clustering coefficient to UMAP's internal k-nearest-neighbor graph can enhance data sensemaking, revealing representative points, dense cores, and tight-knit neighborhoods. This approach unlocks the untapped potential of UMAP's internal graph, offering a practical and complementary method for data exploration that goes beyond the typical focus on 2D embeddings. The authors evaluated their method on MNIST and Fashion MNIST, showing that graph-based analyses are competitive with or complementary to purpose-built methods like k-medoids and HDBSCAN.

**Background**: UMAP is a popular dimensionality reduction technique that constructs a k-nearest-neighbor graph internally to capture the data manifold. This paper demonstrates that analyzing that graph directly with standard network science algorithms can yield valuable insights.

## [ABot-N1: A Slow-Fast Architecture for Visual Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ABot-N1 introduces a slow-fast architecture with dual visual-language signals that decouples cognition from control, enabling explicit Chain-of-Thought reasoning and pixel-goal generation for robust navigation. It achieves state-of-the-art results, including a 35.0% improvement in POI arrival (to 77.3%) and 95.4%/92.9% success rates in complex indoor/outdoor scenes. This work addresses key limitations in visual language navigation foundation models, such as coordinate drift and lack of interpretability, by providing a transparent and generalizable framework. It sets new benchmarks in urban-scale navigation and releases open-source benchmarks, potentially accelerating progress in embodied AI. The slow-fast architecture consists of a slow vision-language reasoner that performs Chain-of-Thought reasoning and outputs a pixel goal, and a fast action expert that uses both textual cues and pixel guidance to generate continuous waypoints. The approach unifies multiple tasks including point-goal, object-goal, POI-goal, instruction-following, and person-following.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Visual Language Navigation (VLN) foundation models aim to unify deep reasoning for grounded spatial decisions across diverse embodied tasks. Previous monolithic policies often suffer from coordinate drift, poor handling of long-tail semantics, and lack of interpretability. The slow-fast architecture is inspired by cognitive science, separating deliberate reasoning (slow) from reactive control (fast).

Researchers propose a zero-setup deep learning framework for multi-phase segmentation of synchrotron X-ray tomography data that generates interpretable masks without user input or retraining during deployment. This framework eliminates manual thresholding and material-specific model training, enabling rapid, near-real-time interpretation of tomography data during beamline experiments, which can accelerate high-throughput materials characterization. The framework combines a material-agnostic mask preparation strategy with a pretrained semantic segmentation network, producing masks for background, sample, bright, dark-gray, light-gray, and porosity regions. It can generate diagnostic-level segmentations within minutes of reconstruction and supports later manual refinement or fine-tuning for application-specific models.

Researchers propose Jetson-PI, a method using foresight-aligned asynchronous correction to deploy Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models on NVIDIA Jetson Orin edge devices, achieving 8.66x and 5.41x improvements in control frequency over naive PyTorch and vla.cpp, respectively. This work addresses the critical challenge of deploying computationally heavy VLA models on low-power onboard devices, enabling real-time robot control in resource-constrained environments, which is essential for practical robotics applications. Jetson-PI introduces a lightweight future correction module to predict future environment representations, and a confidence-based scheduling optimization to balance VLM and action expert invocations, along with system-level accelerations like CUDA graph reuse and GPU-resident intermediate buffering.

## [Privacy-Preserving Edge-Cloud LLM Inference Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13093) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new privacy-centric edge-cloud collaborative inference framework for large language models is proposed, using endpoint-authenticated KV cache and AES-GCM encryption to protect user data. This framework addresses the critical trilemma of latency, hardware constraints, and privacy in LLM inference, enabling efficient and secure deployment on heterogeneous edge devices while reducing per-token latency by up to 46.1% and downlink payloads by 67.4%. The framework offloads decoder inference to the cloud while keeping lightweight modules, draft parameters, and cache access policies local; all transmitted data is quantized and encrypted with AES-GCM, and it supports CPU-only, GPU-equipped, and embedded devices via ONNX deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: On-device LLM inference faces a trilemma: cloud inference exposes user data, on-device inference lacks compute power, and split inference can leak intermediate representations. The proposed framework uses endpoint-authenticated KV cache to ensure only authorized endpoints participate, and AES-GCM encryption for data confidentiality and integrity. Speculative decoding is employed to accelerate inference by having a small draft model propose tokens that a larger model verifies.

## [MGDT: Diffusion Transformer with MoE for Multimodal KG Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15592) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MGDT introduces a novel align-then-diffuse paradigm for multimodal knowledge graph completion, using a Relation-Adaptive Semantic Routing Mixture-of-Experts (RASR-MoE) to select relation-relevant features and a frozen Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) to align multimodal representations before diffusion-based denoising. This work addresses key limitations of existing diffusion-based MKGC methods by decoupling relation-aware feature selection and cross-modal alignment from the denoising process, leading to state-of-the-art performance on three benchmarks. It demonstrates the potential of combining diffusion transformers with mixture-of-experts and large language models for structured knowledge reasoning. MGDT consists of three components: RASR-MoE for relation-adaptive feature routing, a frozen MLLM for semantic alignment, and a Knowledge Graph Diffusion Transformer (KGDT) for graph-conditioned denoising. Experiments on three benchmark datasets show consistent improvements over strong baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Knowledge Graph Completion (MKGC) aims to infer missing entities in a knowledge graph by leveraging structural, textual, and visual information. Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise data from random noise, and Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) replace traditional U-Net backbones with transformer architectures for better scalability. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a technique that uses multiple specialized sub-networks (experts) with a gating mechanism to selectively activate relevant experts for each input, improving efficiency and performance.

## [AuEmoChat: Authentic Emotion in Conversational Speech Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15755) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AuEmoChat introduces a framework for conversational speech synthesis that uses discrete emotion tokens learned via finite scalar quantization, along with an emotion-guided token merging algorithm, to generate more authentic emotional speech. This work addresses the limitations of predefined emotion categories and redundant multimodal tokens in existing CSS systems, potentially improving human-agent interaction in applications like virtual assistants and customer service. The framework includes AuEmoCodec for learning discrete emotion tokens, AuEmoToMe for merging redundant tokens while preserving emotion context, and Authentic Emotion Flow Matching for rendering speech conditioned on merged context and target emotion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational Speech Synthesis (CSS) aims to produce speech with human-like emotion and contextual consistency. Traditional methods rely on a small set of predefined emotion categories (e.g., seven basic emotions), which fail to capture the nuance of authentic human emotions. Token merging is a technique that reduces sequence length by combining similar tokens, improving efficiency without sacrificing quality.

<a id="item-211"></a>
## [Boundary-Seeking Distillation Fails in Bottlenecked Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15919) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that boundary-seeking distillation objectives, such as those used in Contrastive Abductive Knowledge Extraction (CAKE), are fundamentally ill-posed for bottlenecked generative architectures like autoencoders, based on experiments on the MNIST dataset. This finding reveals a critical limitation in applying data-free knowledge distillation to generative models, potentially redirecting research toward manifold-aware synthesis methods that avoid gradient conflicts. The paper reformulates continuous reconstruction as a dense per-feature classification task to enable direct comparison, and shows that independently sampling contrastive targets for coupled decoder outputs violates the latent manifold geometry, causing severe gradient conflicts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Data-free knowledge distillation aims to transfer knowledge from a teacher to a student without original training data. CAKE achieves this for classifiers by generating synthetic samples near decision boundaries. Autoencoders, however, have a bottleneck that couples feature-level classifiers, making boundary-seeking objectives problematic.

## [Modeling Sociocultural Influences on Opinion Formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15968) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new agent-based model explores how word-of-mouth dynamics and mass media interact to shape opinions in segmented societies, with potential implications for social automation engineering. This research highlights how the interplay of social interactions and media exposure can be exploited for mass control, raising ethical concerns for AI and social systems design. The model incorporates agents from different occupational or cultural groups, with opinions formed via within-group observation, cross-group word-of-mouth, and global mass media. The degree of exposure to each source leads to diversified opinion outcomes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Opinion dynamics models study how individuals' opinions evolve through social influence. Agent-based modeling simulates interactions of autonomous agents to understand emergent social phenomena. Word-of-mouth refers to informal communication, while mass media provides broad, one-to-many information dissemination.

<a id="item-213"></a>
## [DPNeXt: Lightweight Decoder for Efficient Multi-Task Dense Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16012) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DPNeXt introduces a lightweight multi-scale feature fusion decoder with boundary guidance that replaces the standard Dense Prediction Transformer (DPT) for efficient multi-task dense prediction using frozen vision transformers. This work significantly reduces computational cost and parameter count while maintaining or improving accuracy, making multi-task dense prediction more practical for resource-constrained robotics and edge devices. DPNeXt-S reduces trainable parameters by 78.6% compared to standard DPT and achieves the fastest inference speed on laptop hardware. The Multi-Task Boundary Guidance (MTBG) strategy improves geometric consistency without extra annotation or inference cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-task dense prediction involves simultaneously performing tasks like semantic segmentation and depth estimation from a single image. Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) serve as powerful feature encoders, but existing decoders like DPT are computationally heavy. DPNeXt addresses this bottleneck with a streamlined decoder design.

## [BERT-Based Zero-Shot Dialogue State Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16021) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a novel BERT-based framework for zero-shot multi-domain dialogue state tracking, enabling generalization to new domains without additional training. It addresses the scalability challenge in task-oriented dialogue systems by reducing the need for domain-specific training data, which is crucial for systems like Google Assistant and Alexa that support many services. The model is evaluated on the schema-based dialogue (SGD) dataset and shows significant improvement over previous baselines in zero-shot settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Dialogue state tracking (DST) is a core component of task-oriented dialogue systems that estimates user goals and constraints at each turn. Zero-shot learning allows models to handle unseen domains without training data, leveraging pre-trained language models like BERT.

## [Fovea-Centered Normalization Boosts OCT Retinal Layer Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16065) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a fovea-centered spatial normalization preprocessing method to align OCT volumes into a common anatomical reference, improving cross-domain retinal layer segmentation. It addresses the critical challenge of domain shifts in OCT segmentation, enabling more robust and clinically meaningful retinal analysis for neurodegenerative disease research. The method is inspired by neuroimaging normalization practices and was evaluated on multiple deep learning architectures using overlap-based, topology-aware, and thickness-based metrics, including a novel ground-truth-free topology violation metric.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 20, 04:00

**Background**: Retinal layer segmentation in OCT is essential for extracting biomarkers but suffers from speckle noise, artifacts, and domain shifts across datasets. Deep learning models often fail to generalize across heterogeneous clinical populations and acquisition protocols.
